*\n789|*************************************************/\n790|\n791|/* For debugging the pre-scan, this code, which outputs the parsed data vector,\n792|can be enabled. */\n793|\n794|static void show_parsed(compile_block *cb)\n795|{\n796|uint32_t *pptr = cb->parsed_pattern;\n797|\n798|for (;;)\n799|  {\n800|  int max, min;\n801|  PCRE2_SIZE offset;\n802|  uint32_t i;\n803|  uint32_t fprintf(stderr, \"META_CAPTURE %d\", meta_arg);\n826|    break;\n827|\n828|    case META_RECURSE:\n829|    GETOFFSET(offset, pptr);\n830|    fprintf(stderr, \"META_RECURSE %d %zd\", meta_arg,  escapes table. */\n854|      if (meta_arg == ESC_g) cc = CHAR_g;\n855|      else for (cc = ESCAPES_FIRST; cc <= ESCAPES_LAST; cc++)\n856|        {\n857|        if (meta_arg == (uint32_t)(-escapes[cc - ESCAPES_FIRST])) break;\n858|        }\n859|      if (cc > ESCAPES_LAST) cc = CHAR_QUESTION_MARK;\n860|      fprintf(stderr, \"META \\\\%c\", cc);\n861|      }\n862|    break;\n863|\n864|    case *\n1086|*************************************************/\n1087|\n1088|/* Compiled JIT code cannot be copied, so the new compiled block has no\n1089|associated JIT data. */\n1090|\n1091|PCRE2_EXP_DEFN pcre2_code * PCRE2_CALL_CONVENTION\n1092|pcre2_code_copy(const pcre2_code *code)\n1093|{\n1094|PCRE2_SIZE *ref_count;\n1095|pcre2_code *newcode;\n1096|\n1097|if (code == This\n1675|    is part of Perl's movement towards a unified syntax for back references. If prop_type/value are not negative, we have a property\n7805|        character type in op_previous. */\n7806|\n7807|        oldcode = code;                   /* Save where we were */\n7808|        code = previous;                  /* Usually overwrite previous item */\n7809|\n7810|        /* If the maximum is zero then the minimum must also be zero; Perl allows\n7811|        this case, so we do too - by simply omitting the item altogether. */\n7812|\n7813|        if (repeat_max == 0) goto END_REPEAT;\n7814|\n7815|        /* Combine the op_type with the repeat_type */\n7816|\n7817|        repeat_type += op_type;\n7818|\n7819|        /* A minimum of zero is handled either as the special case * or ?, or as\n7820|        an UPTO, with the maximum given. */\n7821|\n7822|        if (repeat_min == 0)\n7823|          {\n7824|          if (repeat_max == REPEAT_UNLIMITED) *code++ = OP_STAR + repeat_type;\n7825|            else if (repeat_max == 1) *code++ = OP_QUERY + repeat_type;\n7826|          else\n7827|            {\n7828|            *code++ = OP_UPTO + repeat_type;\n7829|            PUT2INC(code, 0, repeat_max);\n7830|            }\n7831|          }\n7832|\n7833|        /* A repeat minimum of 1 is optimized into some special cases. Otherwise, the original item is\n7835|        left in place and, if the maximum is greater than 1, we use OP_UPTO with\n7836|        one less than the maximum. */\n7837|\n7838|        else if (repeat_min == 1)\n7839|          {\n7840|          if (repeat_max == REPEAT_UNLIMITED)\n7841|            *code++ = OP_PLUS + repeat_type;\n7842|          else\n7843|            {\n7844|            code = oldcode;  /* Leave previous item in place */\n7845|            if (repeat_max == 1) goto END_REPEAT;\n7846|            *code++ = OP_UPTO + repeat_type;\n7847|            PUT2INC(code, 0, repeat_max - 1);\n7848|            }\n7849|          }\n7850|\n7851|        /* The case {n,n} is just an EXACT, while the general case {n,m} is\n7852|        handled as an EXACT followed by an UPTO or STAR or QUERY. */\n7853|\n7854|        else\n7855|          {\n7856|          *code++ = OP_EXACT + op_type;  /* NB EXACT doesn't have repeat_type */\n7857|          PUT2INC(code, 0, repeat_min);\n7858|\n7859|          /* Unless repeat_max equals repeat_min, fill in the data for EXACT,\n7860|          and then generate the second opcode. For a repeated Unicode property\n7861|          match, there are two extra values that define the required property,\n7862|          and mclength is set zero to indicate this. */\n7863|\n7864| mclength;\n7870|              }\n7871|            else\n7872|              {\n7873|              *code++ = op_previous;\n7874|              if (prop_type >= 0)\n7875|                {\n7876|                *code++ = prop_type;\n7877|                *code++ = prop_value;\n7878|                }\n7879|              }\n7880|\n7881|            /* Now set up the following opcode */\n7882|\n7883|            if (repeat_max == REPEAT_UNLIMITED)\n7884|              *code++ = OP_STAR + repeat_type;\n7885|            else\n7886|              {\n7887|              repeat_max -= repeat_min;\n7888|              if (repeat_max == 1)\n7889|                {\n7890|                *code++ = OP_QUERY + repeat_type;\n7891|                }\n7892|              else\n7893|                {\n7894|                *code++ = OP_UPTO + repeat_type;\n7895|                PUT2INC(code, 0, repeat_max);\n7896|                }\n7897|              }\n7898|            }\n7899|          }\n7900|\n7901|        /* Fill in the character or character type for the final opcode. */\n7902|\n7903|        if (mclength > 0)\n7904|          {\n7905|          memcpy(code, mcbuffer, CU2BYTES(mclength));\n7906|          code += mclength;\n7907|          }\n7908|        else\n7909|          {\n7910|          *code++ = op_previous;\n7911|          if (prop_type >= 0)\n7912|            {\n7913|            *code++ = prop_type;\n7914|            *code++ = prop_value;\n7915|            }\n7916|          }\n7917|        }\n7918|      break;\n7919|      }  /* End of switch on different op_previous values */\n7920|\n7921|\n7922|    /* If the character following a repeat is '+', possessive_quantifier is\n7923|    TRUE. For some opcodes, there are special alternative opcodes for this\n7924|    case. For anything else, we wrap the entire repeated item inside OP_ONCE\n7925|    brackets. Logically, the '+' notation is just syntactic sugar, taken from\n7926|    Sun's Java package, but the special opcodes can optimize it.\n7927|\n7928|    Some (but not all) possessively repeated subpatterns have already been\n7929|    completely handled in the code just above. For them, possessive_quantifier\n7930|    is always FALSE at this stage. Note that the repeated item starts at\n7931|    tempcode, not at previous, which might be the first part of a string whose\n7932|    (former) last char we repeated. */\n7933|\n7934|    if (possessive_quantifier)\n7935|      {\n7936|      int len;\n7937|\n7938|      /* Possessifying an EXACT quantifier has no effect, so we can ignore it.\n7939|      However, QUERY, STAR, or UPTO may follow (for quantifiers such as {5,6},\n7940|      {5,}, or {5,10}). We skip over an EXACT item; if the length of what\n7941|      remains is greater than zero, there's a further opcode that can be\n7942|      handled. If not, do nothing, leaving the EXACT alone. */\n7943|\n7944|      switch(*tempcode)\n7945|        {\n7946|        case OP_TYPEEXACT:\n7947|        tempcode += PRIV(OP_lengths)[*tempcode] +\n7948|          ((tempcode[1 + IMM2_SIZE] == OP_PROP\n7949|          || tempcode[1 + IMM2_SIZE] == OP_NOTPROP)? 2 : 0);\n7950|        break;\n7951|\n7952|        /* CHAR opcodes are used for exacts whose count is 1. */\n7953|\n7954|        case OP_CHAR:\n7955|        case OP_CHARI:\n7956|        case OP_NOT:\n7957|        case OP_NOTI:\n7958|        case OP_EXACT:\n7959|        case OP_EXACTI:\n7960|        case OP_NOTEXACT:\n7961|        case OP_NOTEXACTI:\n7962|        tempcode += PRIV(OP_lengths)[*tempcode];\n7963|#ifdef SUPPORT_UNICODE\n7964|        if (utf && HAS_EXTRALEN(tempcode[-1]))\n7965|          tempcode += GET_EXTRALEN(tempcode[-1]);\n7966|#endif\n7967|        break;\n7968|\n7969|        /* For the class opcodes, the repeat operator appears at the end;\n7970|        adjust tempcode to point to it. */\n7971|\n7972|        case OP_CLASS:\n7973|        case OP_NCLASS:\n7974|        tempcode += 1 + 32/sizeof(PCRE2_UCHAR);\n7975|        break;\n7976|\n7977|#ifdef SUPPORT_WIDE_CHARS\n7978|        case OP_XCLASS:\n7979|        tempcode += GET(tempcode, 1);\n7980|        break;\n7981|#endif\n7982|        }\n7983|\n7984|      /* If tempcode is equal to code (which points to the end of the repeated\n7985|      item), it means we have skipped an EXACT item but there is no following\n7986|      QUERY, STAR, or UPTO; the value of len will be 0, and we do nothing. In\n7987|      all other cases, tempcode will be pointing to the repeat opcode, and will\n7988|      be less than code, so the value of len will be greater than 0. */\n7989|\n7990|      len = (int)(code - tempcode);\n7991|      if (len > 0)\n7992|        {\n7993|        unsigned int repcode = *tempcode;\n7994|\n7995|        /* There is a table for possessifying opcodes, all of which are less\n7996|        than OP_CALLOUT. A zero entry means there is no possessified version.\n7997|        */\n7998|\n7999|        if (repcode < OP_CALLOUT && opcode_possessify[repcode] > 0)\n8000|          *tempcode = opcode_possessify[repcode];\n8001|\n8002|        /* For opcode without a special possessified version, wrap the item in\n8003|        ONCE brackets. */\n8004|\n8005|        else\n8006|          {\n8007|          (void)memmove(tempcode + 1 + LINK_SIZE, tempcode, CU2BYTES(len));\n8008|          code += 1 + LINK_SIZE;\n8009|          len += 1 + LINK_SIZE;\n8010|          tempcode[0] = OP_ONCE;\n8011|          *code++ = OP_KET;\n8012|          PUTINC(code, 0, len);\n8013|          PUT(tempcode, 1, len);\n8014|          }\n8015|        }\n8016|      }\n8017|\n8018|    /* We set the \"follows varying string\" flag for subsequently encountered\n8019|    reqcus if it isn't already set and we have just passed a varying length\n8020|    item. */\n8021|\n8022|    END_REPEAT:\n8023|    cb->req_varyopt |= reqvary;\n8024|    break;\n8025|\n8026|\n8027|    /* ===================================================================*/\n8028|    /* Handle a 32-bit data character with a value greater than META_END. */\n8029|\n8030|    case META_BIGVALUE:\n8031|    pptr++;\n8032|    goto NORMAL_CHAR;\n8033|\n8034|\n8035|    /* ===============================================================*/\n8036|    /* Handle a back reference by number, which is the meta argument. We only need the offset to the first occurrence,\n8040|    because if that doesn't fail, subsequent ones will also be OK. */\n8041|\n8042|    case META_BACKREF:\n8043|    if (meta_arg < 10) offset = cb->small_ref_offset[meta_arg];\n8044|      else GETPLUSOFFSET(offset, pptr);\n8045|\n8046|    if (meta_arg > cb->bracount)\n8047|      {\n8048|      cb->erroroffset = offset;\n8049|      *errorcodeptr = ERR15;  /* Non-existent subpattern */\n8050|      return 0;\n8051|      }\n8052|\n8053|    /* Come here from named backref handling when the reference is to a\n8054|    single group (that is, not to a duplicated name). Otherwise we have a problem. */\n8236|\n8237|    default:\n8238|    if (meta >= META_END)\n8239|      {\n8240|#ifdef DEBUG_SHOW_PARSED\n8241|      fprintf(stderr, \"** Unrecognized parsed pattern item 0x%.8x\\n\", *pptr);\n8242|#endif\n8243|      *errorcodeptr = ERR89;  /* Internal error - unrecognized. */\n8244|      return 0;\n8245|      }\n8246|\n8247|    /* Handle a literal character. The code variable is pointing at the code unit\n8375|into which the BRA operator has been stored. This function is used during the\n8376|pre-compile phase when we are trying to find out the amount of memory needed,\n8377|as well as during the real compile phase. The value of lengthptr distinguishes\n8378|the two phases.\n8379|\n8380|Arguments:\n8381|  options           option bits, including any changes for this subpattern\n8382|  xoptions          extra
